I’ve been crying in my truck before work, which is not like me. Every time the phone rings I jump because I think it’s more bad news. And at work I’ve been snapping at the apprentices over little things, then feeling awful about it. I’m making stupid mistakes in the repair bay too, stuff I never would’ve missed before. It’s like my head won’t stay where it’s supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since you started receiving the foreclosure notices?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it trouble sleeping exactly. I mean, some nights I’m tired enough to just drop, but then I start thinking about stupid things, like whether I remembered to put the right socket set back in the bay, or how my dad used to say you can’t fix money problems with a wrench. He had all these sayings.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really been keeping track of my sleep. I just know I don’t feel right lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do since the foreclosure notices started?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to keep pretty busy outside work. Church volunteering, those fitness classes I swore were keeping my knees from turning into rusty hinges, truck-club outings on weekends… I was always going somewhere.\n\nLately I just don’t show up. People probably think I’m being rude, but I just can’t seem to make myself go. And even at work, they’ve taken me out of the high-risk repair bay, which… I know that’s not exactly “fun,” but it was the part I was good at, the part that felt like me. Now I feel like I’m just sort of… standing there, watching my life get smaller.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: I guess… no, not like I used to. I mean, I still try to act normal, but it’s like the things that used to give me a boost don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nThey took me out of the high-risk repair bay at work, and that used to be where I felt useful, you know? Like I knew what I was doing. Now I’m kind of on the sidelines. And outside work… I’ve stopped going to church volunteering, and I haven’t been to my fitness classes or the truck-club outings. I used to keep up with all that. I even had a favorite spot at the fitness place—near the fan, because those rooms get hot as an engine block—but lately I just don’t go. I don’t know if it’s “pleasure” exactly, but I’m not getting much out of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, “hurting myself” sounds so direct, and I don’t sit around making some kind of plan, if that’s what you mean.\n\nMostly I just get tired of all of it, and then my mind jumps around—like one minute I’m thinking about the house, and the next I’m remembering this old dented toolbox my dad had that he refused to throw out, even though the latch barely worked. He said things were still useful if you knew how to hold them right. I don’t know why I keep thinking about stuff like that.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t really been trying to hurt myself. I just feel overwhelmed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been consistently down or depressed most of the time since the foreclosure notices started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, I think so. Ever since those foreclosure notices started showing up, I feel like I’m just coming apart at the seams.\n\nI’ll sit in my truck before work and cry, then wipe my face and go in like nothing’s wrong. And then the phone rings and I jump like it’s bad news again—doesn’t even matter who it is. I’ve been snapping at the apprentices too, which I hate, because some of them are just kids trying to learn, you know? One of them reminds me of my nephew, always asking ten questions before he touches a wrench…\n\nBut, yeah, I’m not myself. I’ve been making stupid mistakes in the repair bay, stuff I never would’ve missed before. It’s like my head won’t stay where it’s supposed to. So if you mean down like… worn out and falling apart, then yes.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since the foreclosure notices started?\n\nPatient: About four months, I think. Since the notices started coming in.\n\nIt’s weird, because at first I kept telling myself it was just a bad week, then maybe a bad month… kind of like when you hear a strange rattle in an engine and convince yourself it’ll settle down on its own. But it didn’t. It’s been pretty much hanging over me for those four months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I try to think of things I used to like, and it’s like my brain just kind of shrugs.\n\nI used to keep pretty busy outside work—church volunteering, those fitness classes, truck-club outings where everyone stands around arguing over parts like it’s a holy debate. There was this one guy who could talk for forty minutes about tire pressure, I swear…\n\nBut I haven’t been showing up for any of that. And work… they actually took me out of the high-risk repair bay, which was kind of humiliating. That bay used to be where I felt useful, you know? Like I knew what I was doing.\n\nSo, I guess… no, I’m not really finding much pleasure in anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge when you're not thinking about the foreclosure notices?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it, because even when I’m not directly thinking, “the house, the notices,” it’s like my body still is.\n\nLike I’ll be in the repair bay and the phone rings, and I jump like I just heard a gunshot. Could be somebody asking about parts, could be my sister sending one of those stupid dog videos—she sends the same kind every week, little bulldog in pajamas or whatever—and still my chest just tightens right up.\n\nEver since those notices started coming, I feel like I’m coming apart at the seams. I’m crying in my truck before work, snapping at the apprentices when they haven’t really done anything, and then I’ll make some dumb mistake I never would’ve made before. So yeah… even when I’m not thinking about it on purpose, I’m on edge.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding people or places because you're worried about what they might think of your situation with the foreclosure notices?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been avoiding people. Not in some big dramatic way, like hiding behind curtains or anything, but I’ve stopped showing up for the things I used to do.\n\nChurch volunteering, my fitness classes, the truck-club outings… I just don’t go. And those truck guys, they’ll ask where I’ve been, and then it turns into, “How’s the house?” or “You get that sorted?” and I can’t stand the look people get, like they’re trying not to pity you.\n\nEven at work, it’s different now. They took me out of the high-risk repair bay, and that… that stings. I used to be trusted with that work. Now I feel like everybody knows I’m not holding it together, whether they say it or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself go through your daily routine?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I mean, I’ve had days where I’m kind of going through the motions, like I drove home and barely remember the drive, but I think everybody does that sometimes, right?\n\nIt’s not like I’m floating up by the ceiling watching myself or anything. Though, funny, my cousin used to say that when she got migraines she felt like her hands weren’t hers, which always sounded terrifying to me… I don’t think I’ve had that.\n\nMostly I just feel tired and distracted, I guess. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I feel disconnected like… like the world isn’t real or I’m not me.\n\nSometimes I’ll be staring at the same wall in the kitchen for too long, or I’ll walk into the parts room and forget what I came in for, but that might just be being tired and distracted. My dad used to do that all the time too—walk into the garage, stand there, swear at himself, then go back inside and remember five minutes later. We used to tease him about it.\n\nSo, I don’t know. I haven’t noticed feeling separated from myself exactly. Mostly I just feel like I’m not paying attention the way I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or detached from what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a daze exactly. I mean, sometimes I’m not fully tuned in, like somebody’s talking and I realize I missed half of what they said… but that could just be me being tired or thinking about ten other things.\n\nDetached, though… I don’t think so. Not like everything around me feels far away or unreal. It’s more like my mind wanders off. Kind of like when you’re working on an engine and your hands know the routine but your head’s somewhere else—though I probably shouldn’t compare everything to work, I know.\n\nSo… maybe distracted, but I haven’t really noticed feeling detached from what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the stressful moments when you received the foreclosure notices?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I relive it exactly. I mean, I remember opening them, sure, and that feeling in my stomach, but it’s not like I’m back there in the room again or seeing it happen like a movie.\n\nSometimes paperwork in the mail makes me tense, but then again, mail’s been a mess for years—my neighbor used to get half my bills, and once I got a catalog for tractor parts addressed to a guy who hadn’t lived there in ten years. I don’t know why I remember that.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say I’m reliving the foreclosure notices. More like… I think about them, and then I wish I wasn’t thinking about them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body or like you're in a dream?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had dreams that felt real before, but not while I’m awake, if that’s what you mean.\n\nAnd outside my body… no, I can’t say I’ve noticed that. Sometimes I feel like I’m just going through motions, like when you’ve done the same repair a hundred times and your hands are ahead of your brain, but I’m still there. I’m still me. I’m not watching myself from across the room or anything.\n\nSorry, I keep comparing things to work. It’s just the easiest way I know how to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my thoughts run around a lot, like I’ll start thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I paid the electric bill or if I left a wrench somewhere at work, and then I’m irritated because I forgot what I was doing.\n\nBut not like… somebody else is steering me, if that’s what you mean. My actions are mine. I might be distracted or kind of on autopilot sometimes, but I don’t feel like I’m not in control.\n\nSorry, I don’t know if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about your situation with the foreclosure notices?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worthless. Guilty… maybe sometimes, but then I start picking apart everything I could’ve done different, and it’s hard to tell if that’s guilt or just me being mad at myself.\n\nLike, I’ll think, if I’d opened that first notice sooner, or made the call before lunch instead of after, or kept better track of things… but then I remember there were bills coming in from all over, and half the time envelopes look the same. I once threw out an insurance form because I thought it was junk mail, and then spent two hours digging through the trash, which was just—anyway.\n\nSo, yeah, I feel bad about it. I feel responsible in pieces, I guess. But worthless? No… I don’t think I’d say that. It’s more like I keep replaying what I should’ve handled better.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're a failure because of your situation with the foreclosure notices?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, sometimes. Not like I think I’m a complete failure as a person, but since those foreclosure notices started coming, I feel like I’m coming apart at the seams.\n\nI’ll be sitting in my truck before work just crying, which is… embarrassing. I usually pull myself together before I go in, because you can’t walk into the bay like that, not with apprentices watching you. And then the phone rings and I jump like it’s going to be another bad call, and I end up snapping at people over stupid little things.\n\nLast week I made a dumb mistake on a repair I’ve done a hundred times, and that’s not like me. So yeah, when that happens, I start thinking, “What is wrong with me?” and it feels like failing, I guess. Not just with the house stuff, but with keeping myself together.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless as a person, not just because of the foreclosure situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. I mean, that word feels pretty big. Worthless as a person? No, I don’t sit around thinking that exactly.\n\nI get down on myself, sure. Like I’ll mess something up and then remember five other things I messed up, and suddenly I’m thinking about this old instructor I had in college who used to say, “Measure twice,” and I still hear his voice sometimes when I’m rushing—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I feel worn down and disappointed in myself sometimes, but I don’t think I’d say I feel worthless. It’s more like I’m frustrated with how I’m handling everything."
